ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u4	Thank you so much for saying that, Mr. President. Dear colleagues and colleagues! In our parliamentary group, we are surprised to hear the amendment of the Law on the Ratification of the European Charter on Regional or Minority Languages because no one has consulted with the organisations of the national communities or our parliamentary group on the amendment before the adoption of the proposal by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia. Let me recall that the Law on Self-Governmental National Communities clearly stipulates that the Government or the proposer of the amendment of the law is still obliged to consult representatives of the national communities in the course of drafting the text of the proposal when it comes to the content directly relating to their rights. In a specific case, it is specifically about the content that falls within this framework. That is why, in particular because of the warning of procedural inconsistencies, my colleague Battelli and I will abstain from voting. However, this lack of consistency of the proposer is only one procedural reason for our retained position. Secondly, an essential substantive question is whether the transfer of competences from the Ministry of Foreign Affairs to the Office of Nationality, as set out in this proposal, is an appropriate way to address such a difficult task. I take it that the Republic of Slovenia ratified the European Charter on Regional or Minority Languages in 2000. In Article 3 of the Law on Ratification, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs has set up the Ministry of Foreign Affairs for the purpose of implementing that instrument. Following the subsequent information provided by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs to our Group, the problem so far was that the Ministry did not seem to have the necessary national powers to implement the European Charter. In their view, this was also clearly confirmed by the four rounds of review of the implementation of the provisions adopted in the dialogue process with the Council of Europe Committee of Experts. They also point out that the Ministry does not have leverage to implement the recommendations of the Committee of the European Charter on Regional and Minority Languages to improve implementation received by the Council of Europe Committee of Experts, which is why the recommendations that the Republic of Slovenia has not implemented in the light of the above are largely repeated throughout the cycle. This is supposed to be the most important reason for the proposed amendment of the law on ratification. As we have been subsequently informed, representatives of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs have consulted the Nationality Office, which has competence to monitor the implementation of the legal and constitutional provisions on the protection of national communities, the preparation of proposals and initiatives to the Government and other national authorities and the preparation of analyses and reports covering broader issues of protection of national communities. They believe that the Nationality Office would be a more appropriate implementing body for this task. This should follow the model established in the framework of the Council of Europe Convention on the Protection of Minorities and National Communities, where the Office of Nationality is listed as the bearer authority involved with the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. In the current term of office, it has been achieved that the Nationality Office acts as a single body within the Cabinet of the President of the Government. From this point of view, a proposal to amend the law can also be adopted positively, but we must be aware, at the same time, that there is a large gap in practice in the field of implementation, although there is a good example of regulated rights of national communities. This is particularly pointed out by the Committee of Experts of the Council of Europe. The question therefore arises whether the Office actually has the necessary human resources capabilities to carry out such an important task. Although the applicants claim that the proposal for a law amending the law on the ratification of this document is entirely technical in nature, we are very reserved in the national community. We do not understand in any way the urgency of approaching such changes at the last moment of the mandate. Nevertheless, we hope that our reservations will prove unfounded. In fact, we also wish to do so, but we note the procedural inconsistencies and possible substantive problems due to the proposed change. So once again, as a sign of our restraint, we will abstain in this vote with Mr Battelli. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u8	Thank you very much, President. Dear colleagues and colleagues! In the Parliamentary Group, Positive Slovenia we agree that public spending should be rationalised and the budget of the state sector balanced and public debt reduced gradually, but we believe that only a fiscal rule will not bring Slovenia out of the crisis. For Positive Slovenia, the fiscal rule is acceptable only under the condition of the precise definition of Slovenia's development programme. Since Article 148 of the Constitution has already been amended by the National Assembly, the adoption of the implementing law is essential, and the question remains open, how it will be and how restrictively it will be written. The amendment to Article 148 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia meant a signal to the international markets that we are serious about our strategy, development and savings. We do not want the start of the implementation of the fiscal rule to require measures to reduce the deficit, which would be too drastic to prevent the economic break-up of Slovenia. We also believe that the drafting of the implementing law is within the power of the Government, and we regret that the Government has not yet been able to carry out these tasks. The time-table chosen by the SDS Parliamentary Group for the introduction of the law is just before the elections, which is extremely unfortunate. In addition to the adoption of the Fiscal Rule Act, it will be necessary to amend and supplement the Public Finance Act, which will specify the fiscal framework. Pension and health reform and reform of the entire social security system will be required. These are reforms that will ensure the long-term sustainability of public finances. The opinion of Members and Members of Positive Slovenia is that this task must be left to the future government. It is therefore appropriate to include the fiscal rule law in this package. In Positive Slovenia, we believe that the key to a better future is not over-saving and tightening the belt. In doing so, we think of the country as well as our citizens and citizens. The key to a better future and consolidation of public finances is to restore the trust of the social partners, the professional public and citizens. The key is the establishment of competent and responsible management bodies of companies, in particular those which are predominantly state-owned. Successful management of state assets will ensure that the country, as its owner, has appropriate relations to support development activities. Slovenia's economic breakthrough requires the launching of large infrastructure projects – we have already warned in Positive Slovenia many times – which will bring the necessary jobs into the wider construction sector, at the same time strengthen Slovenia's territorial strategic position and ensure more coherent regional development. This is what we keep reminding in Positive Slovenia. We have all of this in Positive Slovenia, not least in the programme with which we won the elections in 2011, and we do not deviate from these goals.

Let me mention some of the signals and important emphasis these days on our European and international environment. First, Mr Reza Moghadam, Head of the European Department of the International Monetary Fund, said these days: "A consistent reduction in budget deficits reduces the efficiency of public investment and works pro-cyclically, which all prolongs the impact of the recession." Our fellow socialists in the European Parliament just yesterday, the European Commission's candidate Jean-Claude Juncker, made it clear to him that he has our support if the European Union and the European Commission have loosened their commitments in accordance with strict public financial rules. This does not mean that socialists and social-democrats across Europe are not prepared to respect the signed fiscal pact, but that we intend to act in an anti-cyclical manner when implementing the provisions of the fiscal pact, in order to accelerate economic growth and increase employment. German Federal Minister of Economy and Vice Chancellor Sigmar Gabriel also said these days that no one wanted a greater debt. However, the deficits, the high deficits of countries can only be reduced if we return to the path of economic growth. Respected and respected! This is crucial, and we are all responsible. Some say that the only important thing for the economy and for economic growth is that we have as low taxes as possible and as few administrative barriers as possible. This logic is wrong in our profound belief. We do not have a social security system in this country because it is dedicated to itself, but to help those people who cannot survive alone on the market or those who fail in their path who are in distress. Respected and respected! The proposed fiscal rule in the SDS law is stricter than the commitments we have signed as a country with the signing of a fiscal compact. Social Democrats are committed to respect the constitutional norm, and this is to make sure that Slovenia balances its public finances wisely in the medium term. But let us do this in a way that will increase the possibilities of our economy, which will increase employment opportunities, because this is the only way to end up managing public finances. Let's not make public finances the first one we think of at a time when we have to face a crisis. Let us first think about how we will increase the chances of increasing public finances through increased economic growth. ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u12	Your Honor! Today we will discuss fiscal rules for a long time. The fiscal rule that we wrote down in the Constitution at the end of May 2013. However, in the text of the constitutional law, we wrote that the implementing law should be adopted within six months and that the fiscal rule should be applied for the first time in the preparation of the 2015 budget. So we cannot deny that we are late with the implementing law itself, but we can say that the principle of fiscal rule was followed when the 2015 budget was adopted, as you know, when we adopted it on 15 November 2013. At the time of the budget, the Law on the implementation of the budgets of the Republic of Slovenia for the years 2014 and 2015 was also adopted, which also provided for the appropriate consumption of municipalities and which has also received major criticism from today's proponents. Let's see what the fiscal rule is. Today we'll probably hear about the golden fiscal rule. What's the difference? Fiscal rule or gold fiscal rule? There was a lot of talk about it. Of course, the difference is between one and the other. The Golden Rule limits the responsibility for current needs and, in principle, allows for debt for investment purposes, while only the fiscal rule provides for balanced revenue and budget expenditure without debt. And we're talking about the fiscal rule, not the golden fiscal rule. We are talking about the need to balance income and expenditure, not just the state budget, but also the pension and health budgets. The budgets of the municipalities must also be balanced and the amount of the debt also includes the debt to the obligations of today's SDH or former Soda, Kad and, of course, Dars and DUTB. So, speaking of the fact that only the state budget is the one concerned by the fiscal rule is incorrect and incorrect. With only the implementation of the fiscal rule, the adoption of the implementing law will cover all areas of the country where the country or local community is also the dominant owner. Why are we stressing this only fact? In the Alliance of Alenka Bratusek, we do not oppose the fiscal rule itself. Moreover, we believe that fiscal rule is one of the key building blocks of stable and balanced public finances. This is not the last thing we've written into our program. Respected, as the preceding reference puts it, the decision to be a fiscal rule is not something to be taken so easily. It will be swallowed up in all times of social life, so we are assuming that such an implementing law is adopted by a two-thirds majority in Parliament. It is also desirable to reach as broad a consensus as possible on its personality and content as possible with other stakeholders. However, you can say that, honourable applicants, that you have achieved, you have reached agreement, and if I limit myself to Parliament that you have reached the required two-thirds consensus. The Minister of Finance has called and referred to coordination on the subject of fiscal rules. We have been invited to represent all parliamentary parties, and all parliamentary parties have participated in the coordination. And what was the capture of the coordination itself? The latest information is that there are still open points. Let's start with the fiscal world. The Fiscal Council, which, in our view, is a key supervisory independent body which, according to the interpretation of one, is expected to have more operational powers in relation to the performance itself or the cessation of exceptional circumstances. According to other interpretations of these powers, I should practically not have at all. According to one explanation, the fiscal council should be appointed by a two-thirds majority and, secondly, it should not be regulated within the fiscal rule law. The European Union, just to remind itself, expects and demands that the Fiscal Council be able to deliver independent assessments and opinions on public financial policy. However, the Government should respect such assessments and opinions. Unfortunately, today’s proponents do not really agree with such a request. The question remained also open regarding the determination of exceptional circumstances, the criteria for exceptional circumstances, the extent of possible deviations from the fiscal rule and the determination of the required majority, which would be able to decide on the possible extent of deviations from the fiscal rule. A very important question is what the political consequences should be in the case of deviations from the fiscal rule – well, this is also an issue that I think is still open. Can we vote on the government's confidence or is the constitutional charge in the event of failure to implement the fiscal rule the real sanction? Or is the decision of the presidents of the parties sufficient and to leave to the presidents only the parties to decide how to proceed in the event that the government does not comply with the fiscal rule? Can the government even be responsible for, for example, non-compliance with the rule at local community level? What about the independence of the local community? Next. Somewhere in the proposal for a fiscal rule law that we are considering today, I have also read that the fiscal rule must already be in full force for the 2015 budget. Really? Is that true? The Constitutional Law already states so, but has not the European Union, due to difficult macroeconomic circumstances, set a deadline for achieving the structural balance of the country's budgets? If I am not mistaken, I believe not, this deadline is set for us by the end of 2017. So, misleading on such an important issue as the fiscal rule is by no means in place. I even believe it's harmful, and I also believe that you're not burdened with it. Just a little deceiving here, a little bit there – all to achieve one and a simple goal. I've counted the points that have been left unsettled. They're supposed to be coordinated by the party presidents, and, as I know, they're not. Today, after six hours of discussion, I believe that we will be able to reconcile the essential elements of the implementing law. I'm not sure, so I allow it, of course everything is possible, but I don't believe it's after these first statements or presentations that this would really happen. In the Alliance Group of Alenka Bratušek, we believe that, for all the reasons mentioned above, the issue of the implementing law on fiscal rules is such a difficult subject that it is only right that a decision on this should be taken by a new call from the National Assembly. It is our view that such important issues, which cover virtually all times of life, must be harmonised and adopted by the Government, which has full powers, and confirmed by the National Assembly, which is not in pre-election and during electoral hysteria and noria. Misleading the applicants on the necessity of adopting this law today, as regards the necessity for structural balance of the 2015 budget, as well as the required profitability for Slovenian bonds, which are supposed to be higher-level frauds due to the lack of law. [[...]] None of this is true, of course. The European Union has set a deadline for us until the end of 2017. The required yields keep falling and go up to 3%. However, public debt increased in 2013 due to the rehabilitation of the banking system and slightly increased due to corporate deleveraging. And these are the facts themselves. Therefore, Slovenia's public debt has increased since 2008. If we consider 2013 which was truly one-off in terms of the magnitude of the increase, it increased between the end of 2008 and the end of 2012 by a good 30% of the gross social product, which means that around 8 points of GDP per year are on average throughout the years. During this period, there have been different governments in power, which means that we need to talk about public financial trends without a single action. If we elaborate a slightly more detailed debt reduction strategy and separate it into one-off measures on the one hand and into more lasting debt reduction measures on the other, we can see that both types of measures are described and mapped out by the adoption of a stability programme. The rehabilitation of the banking sector has cost Slovenia about 15% of the gross social product, what has already been done and what is yet to be completed this year. As mentioned above, the rest of the action in the banking sector is foreseen in the stability programme this year because last year, due to the procedures that have been carried out for longer than previously planned, we have not been able to complete. Without further action in the banking sector, the government is planning a government deficit of 3,2 % of GDP in the stability programme. This is exactly the same as planned in last year's stability programme and is fully consistent with the planned gradual fiscal consolidation. We are under an obligation to reduce the deficit of the country's wider sector below 3% of GDP by 2015. However, our public financial strategy is fully in line with the expectations of the European Union, the EU's requirements and our commitments to the EU. Yes, the fiscal rule law should have been passed, but it hasn't been adopted yet. Notwithstanding the fact that this law is not yet in place, public financial policy, as well as the results of the stability programme, is geared towards gradual fiscal consolidation. The government is committed to the gradual fiscal consolidation and is also implementing it through the budgets adopted for 2014 and 2015. This concern, therefore, that public finances will disintegrate without the adoption of this law, is exaggerated and reintroduced only by fraud. For quite some time, the current coalition parties have advocated a solution that the writing of the fiscal rule into the constitution is not necessary at all and that it is sufficient that this rule be written down only in law. It was not until after long-standing convincings and negotiations with the parties of the current coalition that we finally voted with the necessary constitutional majority on 23 May last year to write a fiscal rule into the Constitution as the highest legal act of the State. We have amended Article 148 of the Constitution, which provides that the revenue and expenditure of the state's budgets must be balanced in the medium term without any debt or surplus. The country's budgets are not limited to the state budget, but also to health and pension bills, budgets of local communities and direct users of state and local budgets, which are financed more than 50% from public funds. We have also written down exceptions to when a temporary derogation from the fiscal rule can be granted. These are cases of exceptional circumstances for the country. We have also noted that the criteria for determining exceptional circumstances and the manner of handling in these cases are laid down by the implementing law to be drawn up by the Government and will also have to be voted by the National Assembly by a constitutional majority, a two-thirds majority. I'm afraid we're halfway there. As we have said, we have voted to amend Article 148 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, but we have not adopted a law that would regulate the implementation into the Constitution of the written fiscal rule. The government of Alenka Bratušek, within the 6-month period that expired already last November, has not prepared the implementing law until today and has not invested in the parliamentary procedure, and has not apologized here. That's why the government is sitting on a sad bench. In Novi Slovenija, this treatment is assessed as irresponsible to the country and to the citizens, as well as to the image that Slovenia shows to the outside world. In particular, the European Union, which we are a member of, is closely following us in the field of public finances. We know that every year we need to send the reform programme to the European Commission with the measures we are planning to regulate our public finances. The European Commission also sends its recommendations to us every year in response to the reform programme submitted. And of course, the European Commission made it clear in this year's recommendations that the Government has not prepared its task of drafting a law to implement the fiscal rule. And here we need to make clear the responsibility of the entire current government coalition. It is precisely because of these delays or the passiveness of the Government that we will today consider the draft law on the fiscal rule of Members of the New Slovenia Group. The law provides a substantive explanation and defines the existence of exceptional circumstances when it is possible to derogate from the fiscal rule, in particular defining the powers and tasks of the Government, the National Assembly and other authorities in the event of imbalances in the State budget due to exceptional circumstances. Of course, some solutions need to be complemented and further improved, which can be done in further consideration of this law in the parliamentary procedure. Nevertheless, we Christian Democrats are convinced that by adopting the law we are already so late that no coalition party of the government so far is likely to deny support for this law in this first reading. Thank you very much.

In this first Article, it provided that all revenue and expenditure to finance public spending must be covered by the budgets of the countries and must demonstrate medium-term balance. This sounds very learned, but the fact is that this fiscal rule is in some way already in force in all other public spheres of the country, but it does not clearly apply to the main contractor, that is, the government and the Ministry of Finance. This means that if the Government does not want to make such a law, we are all Members or the National Assembly in some way obliged to arrange it. And it is not right to try or try to translate this obligation into new Members who will come to the National Assembly after 13 July. The fact is, no domestic economy or household can afford to spend more from month to month than it has inflows within its budget. The same applies to all economic operators. Obviously, this rule does not apply to our country or is not respected, although some balance is already described in other laws, which unfortunately, like the constitutional law, are not binding. It is therefore right that this call by the National Assembly should carry out this debate and support the law which it has already adopted, several times in this reference. And there is no argument that this rule and this law would not be supported at this first reading and adopted in the final stage. So I support the adoption of this law.

A number of these figures have already been mentioned in the debate so far, and these different numbers have also been interpreted several times. This legal package also allows larger entrepreneurs and slightly larger companies to pay a fixed tax on their income while at the same time suspending accurate accounting, proof of expenditure and all other red tape. ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u88	President, thank you for your word. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! The possibility of determining the tax base of income from activities, taking into account standard expenditure, is established with a view to administrative simplification for small business entities in compliance with their tax obligations. The system has been in force since the introduction of the Income Act and was substantially renewed in 2012 by applying it for the first time for the tax year 2013. The accounts of the income tax on activities under the amended scheme were submitted to the tax administration by 22 April of this year. This period from 22 April to today is so short that it is impossible to prepare all data from the tax accounts for analysis and determination as to whether the actual reason why it is assessed that the recast scheme has not been implemented in accordance with the 2012 expectations is indeed in the low standardised expenditure or in the low income threshold set by the law. As a result, in the first reading, the Government objected to the proposal of the law, since the proposed solutions do not follow or are not based on an analysis of the actual situation and the realistic findings as to what is the key problem of the so-called flat-rate taxation of income for individual entrepreneurs. Any simplification, including one with flat-rate taxation, means a shift from taxation to actual economic power. And any simplification means inequality between taxpayers. When we look at the segments in which the taxpayers decide to tax under standardised expenditure and to use this system, we find that these are exclusively and only activities in which the actual expenditure is extremely low. And only in these cases can it be expected that this system, as introduced by the bill, will continue to live. If we only look at the amount of revenue that taxpayers have, only 77 % of the 97 000 entities could operate through this system, only 15,000 business entities liable to the activity tax have decided on it. Taking all these facts into account, both in the first and second readings, the Government warned that, before the system that only lives for one year, we should wait for the possibility to analyse the data of the 2013 tax period. However, given that this is not the case and that the National Assembly estimates that the ideas of the Chamber of Commerce and Economics should be followed and that the existing system should be changed immediately, we have proposed some amendments in the second reading to improve the text or make it operational at all. Some of the solutions that we have advocated on the labour market have also been confirmed through the substantively identical amendments of the bill's proponents. We estimate, however, that these texts will not give appropriate guidance to those who will be obliged to ensure the uniform and uniform implementation of the law. For this reason, as a working tool, we have drawn up a proposal for new amendments, first of all we have tried to harmonise them with the drafters, but since we have not been successful in doing so, we have proposed to the other parliamentary groups that they support the amendment proposal. The amendments do not affect the interests of the applicants, but clarify the provision so that the implementation cannot lead to different interpretations and different actions of the tax authorities in the control, and from this point of view the quality of the legal text is improved at least in technical terms. Thank you very much.

Of which 135 in the field of advice. ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u106	Mr President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The law on the conditions of the energy concession of the lower Sava was introduced by the four members of the National Assembly in the previous term, and I was alone among the co-signers of this law. The law regulates the way hydropower plants are built on the lower Savi. The law replaced the law with a similar name that existed before, and three or four hydroelectric plants were built under this law and the construction of the Brežica hydroelectric power plant began. The 2011 Act provides that the infrastructure part of the arrangements for Brežica hydroelectric power plant is financed by the Climate Change Fund. This is a significant change under the legislation in force until then, as by 2011 the main water infrastructure or accumulation pool is to be financed from the so-called Water Fund. Let me explain a little bit that hydropower plants are made up of energy and infrastructure. The energy part is financed by energy, the concessionaire, the hydroelectric company in Lower Savi, the infrastructure part, these are water arrangements, the simplified said accumulation pool, and mainly by 2011 it is financed by the water fund and after 2011 by the Climate Change Fund. This should ensure continuity in financing the construction of infrastructure work, as in the past there have been a number of traps across the country and the pressure on the Water Fund has become relatively large. And all of us who want to work smoothly at the hydro power plants at the bottom of the Sava are aware that there is a need to find a new system for building infrastructure work. We must be aware that the State has granted a concession to the hydroelectric power plant at the bottom of the Sava to carry out certain State commitments from this concession, and the State's specific commitment is to follow the construction of the infrastructure work in energy, that is to say those who are building the energy part. Mrs Alenka Bratušek, current President of the Government on her way out, laid the foundation stone for the construction of the hydroelectric Brežica about a month ago and something else, and today it is more than clear that the energy part of the hydroelectric power plant is being built. However, it has been stuck in financing and providing funding for the continuation of design, land purchases for infrastructure works, that is to say what is the task of the candidates or the country which granted the concession. It is also clear that there is not enough money in the Climate Change Fund to continue construction, although the Climate Change Fund has certain funds, which Eco has dedicated to investing in energy renovation of buildings, this year is specifically two and a half million euros from the Climate Change Fund for the purchase of buses in Ljubljana and Maribor and so on. In short, the Ministry of Environment and Space has also allocated funds from the Climate Change Fund for certain other projects. And since the law in 2014 no longer allows the financing of infrastructure works for hydropower plants from the Water Fund, we propose that it is possible to continue to do so in a transitional period, this year and 2015, to finance, I say, water infrastructure from the Water Fund or other budgetary resources. We do not want to repeat the story from the Krška hydropower plant when the energy part was being built and the construction of the infrastructure work stopped, and the result is that a hydroelectric power plant can be built without an accumulating pool and if the hydropower plant does not operate, because, of course, it cannot operate without an accumulation pool, there is about half a million euros of damage every month because the water flows through instead of running through the turbines. We would therefore like to see the construction of the Brežica hydropower plant, which has already been laid on the foundation stone and is already being built in the energy work, building smoothly in the infrastructure work. We therefore propose and give the Government the opportunity to finance this infrastructure part from several sources, not only from the Climate Change Fund. It may also be from the Water Fund or other budgetary resources. In this way, we want the basic and fundamental purpose of the construction of hydroelectric plants on the lower Sava – in particular it is now the turn of the Brežice hydroelectric power plant, followed by the Mokrice hydroelectric power plant – to be carried out continuously, in accordance with the law adopted.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u108	Honorable President, colleagues and colleagues! The Committee on Agriculture, Forestry, Food and the Environment considered the draft law at its 43rd urgent meeting, on 13.6.2014. The Committee was informed of the opinion of the Legal and Legislative Service, which made a comment on the draft law and is summarised in the amendment of the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party to Article 1. The Committee also took note of the opinion of the Commission of the National Council for Local Self-Government and Regional Development, which supports the proposal for a law. Given that the representative of the Ministry of Agriculture and the Environment opposed the adoption of the bill and that the representatives of the Ministry of Infrastructure and Space supported the proposal of the law, the members of the committee first expressed their disappointment at the fact that the Government had not adopted an opinion on such an important issue, which would make sense and necessary, especially in the fact that the two ministries had different views on the proposed legal solutions. They therefore expressed the expectation that, pending the consideration of the draft law at the meeting of the National Assembly, the Government would nevertheless unify the position of the competent ministries and adopt a single opinion on the proposed amendment to the law. In the discussion below, the representative of the proponents, referring to the Ministry of Agriculture and the Environment's rejection of co-financing due to the indivisible relationship of water and energy infrastructure facilities, pointed out that the legislator had already clearly defined the concept of construction, which is still in force today and on the basis of which three hydropower plants have already been built. It is incomprehensible that the Ministry of Agriculture and the Environment opposes this concept, but has never proposed a different, better solution. It is even more incomprehensible that today, when the fourth hydro power plant is being built, this is the Brežice hydroelectric power plant, so the investment is already being made, the ministry does not know how the agreed concept of construction would be realized. While the applicants recognise that many environmental flood problems need to be solved in the country, flood protections must be built, some may even be more urgent than those on the bottom of the Sava. It is therefore appropriate that the resources of the Water Fund should also be allocated to other projects, but not to projects already under way. In particular, the proposer's representative stressed that the proposed amendment to the law does not mean that funding for infrastructure facilities must be provided from the Water Fund, but that, if there is no other source, it can also be provided from the water fund with the aim of preventing any damage to the infrastructure facility. The proposed law is therefore an emergency exit in the event that the Government will not be able to provide funds from other sources to finance project documentation and build an accumulation pool at Brežica hydropower plant. This indicates that each month the delays in the construction of the Accumulation Pool at the built power plant amount to a circus of EUR 500 000 loss due to the failure to produce electricity. He also recalled that the bulk of the Water Fund is full of concessional fees and water reimbursements paid by hydropower plants, so it is also appropriate that part of the funds be reimbursed. Thus, the Brežice hydroelectric power plant will, once built, repay funds to the water fund by repaying the loans. A member of the Committee from the Positive Slovenia Group also presented the position on the proposed legal solutions. He stressed that the Group supports any investment in infrastructure, including energy, which provides greater economic growth and opens new jobs, but provided that financial resources are provided for such an investment. Due to difficulties in closing the financial structure for the completion of the energy facilities on the bottom of the Sava and the search for an additional source of funding through the Water Fund, the parliamentary group of the bill does not support it. He also drew attention to the fact that 60% of the funds collected from concessions are returned to the municipalities, while the rest of the funds are allocated to the water fund, from which about two-thirds of the funds, that is, some 32 million euros, is allocated annually to flood areas on the Sava River. This means that all those who pay concessions and water refunds in the country contribute two thirds of the funds for flood zones in Sava. In the debate, the Committee identified the amendments tabled by the SDS Group and the SD Group of Members to Article 1. The amendments are identical, according to the content, in that the transitional period with the amendment of the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group is foreseen until 31. 12. 2015, with the amendment of the Social Democrats' Group of Ambassadors until 31. 12. This Decision shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. The Committee adopted the amendment of the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group and did not define itself until the amendment of the Social Democrats' Group, as it became irrelevant. In the light of the amendment adopted, the text of the amended law, which includes the adoption of the amendment, is prepared on the basis of Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended bill is an integral part of the Committee's report.

This amendment seeks to achieve a two-year extension of the transitional period in which it is possible to finance water and energy infrastructure in the construction of hydropower plants on the lower Sava also from the Water Fund or other budgetary resources. In this way, the proposer wishes to ensure the smooth financing of the remaining part of the hydroelectric power plant chain in the lower Sava, which remains the basic source of funding for this infrastructure in the future Climate Change Fund established in accordance with the law governing environmental protection. The energy part of this plant is already being built and the construction of the project documentation for the Accumulation Pool, which would require a circus of €4 million. Already 50% of the funds from the fund are earmarked for the construction of the hydrocentral. There is a lack of funds to build anti-floating security, which some estimates would require some €1.2 billion. We cannot ignore the fact that the more resources we spend on building hydropower plants, the less they are available to regulate watercourses and build flood protection, and we all know that Slovenia is flooding more and more. In addition, we would like to draw attention to the fact that 60% of the funds collected from concessions are returned to the municipalities, while the rest of the funds are allocated to the water fund, from which about two-thirds of the funds, i.e. around EUR 32 million, are allocated annually to flood areas on the Sava River. This means that all those in the country who pay concessions and water refunds contribute two-thirds of the funds for flood zones in Sava. In the DeSUS Group, we advocate any investment in infrastructure that provides greater economic growth and opens new jobs, but provided that such an investment is provided with financial resources. However, this concrete project has serious difficulties in closing the financial structure to complete the energy facilities on the bottom of the Sava. Therefore, in the DeSUS Group, we are not convinced that the right solution is to seek an additional source of funding through the Water Fund and we will, as has already been said, vote in our conscience. The objectives of our energy policy are safe supply, environment and climate-friendly acquisition and use of energy, appropriate and competitive prices. Increasing energy needs, high energy prices, increasing international supply risks and growing dependence on politically unstable areas underline the need for long-term common energy supply concepts. In 2012, the Act on the terms of the concession for the exploitation of the energy potential of the lower Sava replaced the previously applicable law with the same name. The purpose of the two laws was to ensure the construction of hydroelectric power plants on the lower Sava, the fulfilment of certain requirements for flood management, the release of spatial reserves that hindered infrastructure regulation and economic development and strategic guidelines for the supply of electricity from hydro power plants to Slovenia. With the proposed amendment and supplementation of the Act on the terms of the concession for the exploitation of the energy potential of the Lower Sava, it is proposed that the period during which water and energy infrastructure facilities can be financed in an indivisible manner also from the Fund for Water or other budgetary resources be extended by two years, which is until the end of 2015. This will allow for a smooth continuation of the construction of the infrastructure part of Brežica hydropower plant. The proposed funding for the further construction of the water and energy infrastructure in an indivisible relationship from the Water Fund means that the Brežice hydropower plant will begin to restore funds to the Water Fund through part of the concession fee and the water refund and thus start to finance the Water Fund for other flood projects. Since the proposal, in our view, is good and has wider benefits for achieving climate and environmental objectives and for reviving the Slovenian economy, we will support Members from New Slovenia – Christian Democrats.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u128	Chairman, thank you so much for your word and a nice greeting to all present! The proposal for a law that we are considering today primarily covers the area of equal opportunities and non-discrimination of persons with disabilities. As such, it is intended to supplement existing legislation on the prevention of discrimination based on disability. Therefore, the key task of the law is to systematically prevent discrimination on the one hand and to promote equal treatment on the other. Today's amendment to the law shows that despite good solutions to existing legislation, this from 2010, the area of equalization of opportunities for people with disabilities has not yet been optimally regulated. If we are based on the assumption of non-adaptability, the lack of access to the environment, which is often the reason to put people with disabilities in an unequal position and not on the disability of the disabled, solutions are proposed to move in the right direction. The proposed novelty facilitates co-financing of technical devices for disabled persons with sensory disabilities and facilitates co-financing of vehicle adjustment to disabled persons. In doing so, let me say that dogs assistants are considered as technical aids in this case. So far, this has been done through public procurement procedures. There's been quite a few problems here. We know that when it comes to public procurement, it is decided on the basis of the lowest price, and it is not possible to select operators and so on. This amendment is a proposed solution, namely the implementation of valuations that will enable disabled persons to pay for technical aids and to make adjustments to the vehicle. This is also in line with the Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, which obliges countries that have ratified it to respect fundamental principles that include respect for inherent dignity, personal autonomy and freedom of choice and individual independence. The solution so far has shown that beneficiaries are limited in their choice, accessibility and choice of operators. All assistants will help disabled persons to manage their daily responsibilities and tasks by enabling them to become more independent and independent. Thus, these individuals will be more likely to become involved in society. The Allies of Alenka Bratušek will welcome changes and the amendment of the law will be supported. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u134	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! The adoption of the Law on Equalization of Opportunities for Persons with Disabilities in 2010 has taken a major step forward in efforts to ensure and apply equal opportunities for the integration of persons with disabilities in all areas of life. To this end, the law has set out a number of measures. These include co-financing of technical devices and co-financing of vehicle adjustment to disabled persons. The current law provides for the selection of suppliers of technical devices and the selection of vehicle adapters following procurement procedures. The purpose of such an arrangement was to ensure, in the long term, the lowest possible price for technical devices. However, it was found that the price in this way is not necessarily lower and that public procurement limits the choice of beneficiaries and reduces the availability of tools and the selection of vehicle adjustment operators. These two are the first set of changes envisaged by the novel that we are considering today. The DeSUS Group supports the Government's proposal because we agree that the implementation of the valuation system in the payment of approved and co-financed technical aids and the implementation of the technical adaptation of the vehicle is much more favourable to the disabled, because the procedure is simple and means a greater choice of the user himself in the acquisition of the device or in the search of a suitable contractor for the implementation of the vehicle adjustment. Another important change in the law brings a completely new measure which will allow the most difficult and difficult disabled to become fully and effectively involved in society. It is the right to obtain a dog assistant to help a disabled person with daily tasks. The DeSUS Group supports all changes that contribute to improving the quality of life of people with disabilities. This law brings many new and good solutions to people with disabilities and, of course, to their loved ones, because they must all face very different obstacles, which do not allow equal and quality integration of persons with disabilities into everyday life. Simplifying the measures already in place and introducing new ones with this novel greatly improves the opportunities for achieving equal opportunities for people with disabilities, and we therefore welcome the amendments and additions to the law in the DeSUS Group. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u140	Thank you very much. Say good-bye again! The Act on the Equalization of the Possibilities of Persons with Disabilities defines the co-financing of technical aids for persons with disabilities with sensory disabilities which are not eligible under other rules, and in life these devices are needed to overcome communication barriers and to enable a safe and independent life. As the payment of technical aids is facilitated and the adjustments of the vehicle are ensured by the valuations, the procedure will be simpler, easier and more user-friendly for the disabled as it is now. Because of all the changes and the new developments brought by the law, we will unanimously support the law in the SD Group. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u142	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. On the table we have a new Law on Access to Public Information – novel D. As you know, about a few months ago, March, we adopted novel C, and we have not done our homework in full. When we released the data on bad loans D and E, which were transferred to a bad bank, of course we did not follow the next phase, so that we also offered the public information on these same loans D and E, namely those that remained in banks. Of course, in this case, the banking profession rejects such a solution and has a legitimate right, since it is an intervention in banking secrecy, but we believe that banking secrecy cannot be absolute here, because there is a whole range of other public interests that outweigh this banking secrecy. Why, in fact, should we adopt this amendment of the law and ensure that the bad D and E credits left at commercial banks are also subject to public access and public publication? Because the same issues need to be dealt with equally. We have published taxpayers in this country. Why did we publish them? Because the public has the right to know who they are who do not pay taxes and why a budget hole arises. We've published data on bad D and E credits transferred to the DUTB. Why did we publish this information? Because the public has the right to know why and what credits actually created the bank hole and why it was necessary to recapitalise and financially secure the recapitalisation of banks. And, of course, we follow the third step – we also need to open up information to the public about what those D and E loans that have remained on banks that have not been transferred to the so-called bad bank are, and here too, this transparency is needed. Why should we accept this novel? Also because we follow the principle of righteousness. Someone was lucky that bad credit D and E was not transferred to a bad bank and that bank secrecy was supposed to apply to him, and someone else had not had this happiness and was transferred to a bad bank and data on this loan are publicly available. Such access to public data does not impede criminal investigations and prosecution of banking crime can even benefit. We know what the problems of the NPU and the U.S. Attorney's Office are. They don't get data from banks, they hide data from banks, they don't get good criminal charges, they don't get flawed criminal charges, and we actually have a chance here that these things can be processed immediately. Eminent lawyers have said, quote Dr. Juhart: "Data are not crucial to the committees. Bank secrecy is becoming relative, not absolute anymore. States no longer protect absolute bank secrecy," and "The solution is not constitutionally disputed." What does such a solution mean for the competitiveness of the bank, which is subject to the publication of bad loans D and E? The new committees will trust this bank more, as it is clean, recapitalised and subject to ethical behaviour; poor commissioners do not need such a bank; good commentants have nothing to fear, their data will remain secret; transparency in the banking system actually strengthens confidence in the same Slovenian banking system. There was also concern about the legal position of the customer, in which case the borrower was not sufficiently protected, but let us ask ourselves, because it is precisely because of this borrower who did not repay the loan, for whatever reason it is in fact in [[…]] [[End of discussion warning sign]] these problems, that the tax money of this bank also needs to be addressed and require not only responsibility, but also transparency and public access to data from those D and E loans that are actually dead loans in order to identify all the backgrounds of these banking transactions. [[…]] I suggest we back this up. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u144	Thank you, Madam Vice President. That's a good-bye to the rest of us! The Committee on Home Affairs, Public Administration and Local Self-Government, as a parent working body, considered the proposal for a law on amendments and additions at its 36th urgent meeting on 18 June. The law was submitted for consideration by a group of Members with first-signed Richard Branislje. Members and members of the Committee had at their disposal as materials for the meeting the draft Law of 9. 4th this year, opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service of 16. 6. 2014 and the opinion of the Commission of the State Council of 18 6. This Decision shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. No amendments were tabled in the course of business. In its supplementary explanation, Mr Braniselj pointed out that, at the time of the adoption of the Public Information Act in 2003, natural and legal persons were given access to information of a public nature which was available to public authorities, local authorities, public authorities and public authorities and public service operators, and had been recast three times since its adoption. With the latest novelty, the commitment also extended to business entities under the dominant influence of public law. They will become publicly available, inter alia, advisory, sponsoring, donor and author contracts, information on the monthly remuneration of management bodies, management bodies and members of the supervisory authority, the agreed amount of retirement and the total amount of net remuneration paid in the previous year. Important data will be published proactively through publication on the website of the business operator. He pointed out that pro-active transparency through law was also extended to the Company for the Management of Bank Claims, hereinafter the DUTB, which would have to publish a list of non-payers on its website. He pointed out that the ZDJZ-C provides, as publicly available, some of the essential data relating to the bad loans transferred to the DUTB. Therefore, on the basis of the ZDIJZ-C, the data are determined only for non-payments credits, which are conducted as weaknesses in the balance sheets of banks and savings banks classified in Group D and E and transferred from banks to DUTB. In view of the huge financial resources invested in the recapitalisation of banks and the need for certain transparency in the cooperation of DUTB and recapitalised banks, which must ensure their future effective operation and prudent use of funds, he pointed out that there was no reason to distinguish between those poor loans transferred to DUTB and those belonging to the same poor rating group. The Government has not delivered an opinion on the draft law. Representative of the Legal and Legislative Service mag. Sonja Bien Karlovšek pointed out that the PSC gave an opinion on the draft law in terms of its compliance with the Constitution, the legal system and the legislative and technical aspects. She pointed out that since its adoption in 2003, the ZDIJZ has been renovated three times and the latest novel has expanded the commitment circle. It pointed out that the proposal for a law intended to provide for free access also in respect of the remaining, untransferted loans of non-payments remaining in the bank and which were granted a measure under the law governing measures to strengthen the stability of banks. However, they represent precisely those transactions where access to the decisions referred to in Article 4a to other business entities under the dominant influence of public law persons is not necessary because they are transactions concluded by business entities in respect of their services by their customers. It stressed that such a selective approach to these banks constitutes a derogation from the principle regime whereby information relating to the core activity of business entities under the dominant influence of public law persons, i.e. with regard to users of their services or customers, will not be made public because they do not fall within the information referred to in Article 4a(1). Among other things, it pointed to the systemic regulation of this topic, as set out in the Banking Act. It also highlighted a warning from the European Central Bank. The President of the Commission of the National Council for State Regulations, Rajko Fajt, stressed that the Commission supports the proposal for a law. In the debate, Members who supported the adoption of the bill stressed that the extensive recapitalisation of banks with taxpayers' money, the transfer of bad loans to the DUTB and the continued operation of recapitalised banks represent exceptional economic conditions that require high transparency limits. On the other hand, some MEPs have pointed out the many concerns that the ZPS has raised regarding the proposed provisions in its opinion. In particular, legal and systemic derogations and constitutional concerns regarding the provision of access to information outside the public sector bodies have been highlighted. They considered that the adoption of the proposed decisions and the publication of confidential customer data could harm banks and make their competitive situation worse irreparably. The Committee then adopted its amendments to Articles 1, 2 and 4. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the Supplemental Proposal for a Law, which includes the amendments adopted, is prepared on the basis of Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended bill is an integral part of this report. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u146	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Colleagues and colleagues! On 12 December 2013, the Bank of Slovenia and the Government of the Republic of Slovenia presented the results of an independent review of eight Slovenian banks, representing a representative sample of 65% of the Slovenian banking system. The results of this review have shown that the capital deficit of the banks examined or the so-called banking hole is EUR 4.7 billion. On 18 December 2013, an invoice was issued to taxpayers for the recapitalisation of the New Ljubljana Bank, NKBM, Banka, Probanka and Factor Banka totalling EUR 3.2 billion in tax money. And I believe we haven't stopped at this point. In the Civil Gazette, we were not thrilled with the recapitalisation, but there was no other solution to the situation. We are aware that if banks do not begin to properly service their economies as soon as possible, there is no hope of recovery and a new start. In the National Assembly, we did not just observe it. On 24 March this year, we, colleagues and colleagues jointly adopted a new Law on Access to Public Information, which introduces, inter alia, pro-active transparency in relation to poor loans transferred to the Company for the Management of Bank Claims or the so-called Bad Bank. In accordance with the above-mentioned amendment, the Bad Bank published on its website a list of bad loans transferred from NLB and NKBM on 17 April this year. State banks and also Banka Slovenije have already pointed out that the publication of these data will cause great damage to banks and the economy and none of this has happened. In view of the huge financial resources invested in the recapitalisation of these banks, the need for greater transparency in the operation of recapitalised banks is certainly justified. It is also justified to disclose to the public certain data on those bad loans of the same rating that were not transferred to a bad bank and remain in these recapitalised banks. This will also ensure the effective functioning and prudent use of resources in the future. This bill therefore eliminates the distinction between those bad loans that have been transferred to a bad bank and those that belong to the same bad rating group and have not been transferred to a bad bank. The recapitalised banks or banks that have received measures to strengthen the stability of banks, like a bad bank, will have to proactively publish on their website information on all the bad credits they will maintain in their balance sheets on the day of the law's entry into force. We are talking about loans where the borrower is generally late to pay for more than 90 days, which are non-solvent, for which an application has been made before the competent court for the initiation of a compulsory settlement or bankruptcy procedure. To put it simply, it is loans from non-payers of legal persons, dead capital in these banks. We will take into account the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service, at the same time we are aware that this is not the first and not the last recapitalisation of Slovenian banks, so we will act preventively and will give credit committees, members of the Boards of Supervisory Boards of Banks and all those involved in the policy of lending, a clear signal that they will act legally, responsibly and economically, otherwise they will not only be under the care of the police, prosecutors, shareholders, but of all citizens and citizens. The aim of the novelty is to ensure that as little tax money as possible will be spent in the future because someone in the lending policy has not been sufficiently economical and consistent. The measures taken so far by the Bank of Slovenia, the Ministry of Finance, the various regulators of the banking sector have focused primarily on putting out fires and not addressing the causes. With the amendment of the law, we will add a small, but not a small, small, insignificant piece to the mosaic of better, more responsible and more transparent bank management. Members and Members of the National Gazette will be supported.

These decision makers must first be identified by their name and function and then called to account, criminally and materially. ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u155	Thank you very much. On behalf of the Parliamentary Group of Positive Slovenia, Ms Alenka Bikar will present her position.
ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u156	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Dear fellows and fellows, welcome! The recently adopted amendment to the Public Information Access Act has, through its implementation, brought a wider range of public information commitments and thus a significantly larger set of information and information available to the general public. In the Group of Positive Slovenia, we supported the latest amendment, but we pointed out at the time that measures must be proportionate and should contain safeguards against abuse. That is why we have not already supported the amendment proposed by the National Gazette, which envisaged the same solutions as proposed by the proposed amendments to the law before us. In addition to the publicly available essential data relating to the bad loans transferred to the Bank's Claims Management Company, the proposed bill also intends to provide for free access in relation to the remaining outstanding loans of non-payers remaining on the bank receiving measures under the law governing measures to strengthen the stability of banks. According to the applicants, there is no reason to distinguish between those bad loans transferred to the DUTB and those belonging to the same bad rating group, D and E and not transferred to the DUTB. At this point in the Group of Positive Slovenia, we point out that this is precisely the situation, whether credit has been transferred to the DUTB or not, as the Legislative and Legal Service also points out in its opinion. Credits classified in group D and E are not only related to banks that receive measures to strengthen stability, but can be led by other banks as loans to non-payments, i.e. as weaknesses in the balance sheets. In doing so, the legal position of the bank's client is neglected, which had no effect on the fact that the bank was receiving measures to strengthen the stability of the banks. Disclosure of information relating to so-called bad loans would thus have a negative impact on the competitiveness of these persons and also on the competitive position of the bank on the market, since, in principle, under the Banking Act, all data, facts and circumstances on the individual party that the bank must protect regardless of the way in which it obtained these data are confidential. At this point, we draw attention to the opinion of the European Central Bank, which underlines that the Capital Requirements Regulation, which lays down at EU level a comprehensive disclosure regime, allows institutions to waive disclosure of information if information is confidential if institutions have obligations towards parties or in relation to parties to counterparties that they commit to confidentiality. However, confidentiality clauses are usually found in loan contracts and thus form a binding relationship with a client or counterparty. The proposed law would thus affect the confidentiality of the relationship between the bank and the customer and the competitive market relationship, since the bank receiving measures to strengthen stability could suffer damage or incur an irreparable deterioration in its competitive situation by disclosing the data relating to outstanding loans. The same applies to the position of its client. Finally, in the Positive Slovenia Group, we are asked how, with the public, credit data that is or will remain in banks will be achieved the purpose pursued by the proposer with the bill, this is to ensure future and effective functioning and the prudent use of DUTB funds and recapitalised banks. The proposed law is intended to make it public only with regard to loans, which, at the date of entry into force of the bill, are kept as bad loans in their balance sheets. For all the reasons mentioned above, we will reject the proposal of the law in the Parliamentary Group of Positive Slovenia. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u162	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. Colleagues and colleagues! This morning a good, professional and high-quality debate on the proposal for a declaration on constitutional aspects of the implementation of the Constitutional Court decision on the Municipality of Ankara was held on the home committee. In addition to Members, Members and representatives of the Communities, two eminent legal experts participated. During the debate, Dr Grad also mentioned that he did not know the case in a world where the Constitutional Court in the way that we are dealing with today would intervene in the work and decision of the legislative branch of power. The declaration consists of the signatories, Members and Members of Parliament, taking solely for the defence and respect of the Constitution and the legislative functions of the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia. The defence of the Constitution and of the functions of the National Assembly should be the primary task of the Constitutional Court. However, since it was in this case that the Constitutional Court overturned the law adopted by the National Assembly, it exceeded its powers and extended its power to the legislative branch and thereby violated the Constitution. One of the constitutional lawyers even pointed out that the Constitutional Court had taken this decision too far. It is therefore essential that Members and Members defend the integrity of the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia, which is given to us by the Constitution in Article 139. If Members and Members did not respond to the Constitutional Court's decision, they would leave the Constitutional Court with a legislative function, which could mean the beginning of the end of democracy in our country, since this act would no longer apply to the principle of the division of power, which is enshrined in Article 3 of the Constitution. According to the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia, the National Assembly is still solely responsible for amending laws and enacting laws, and this competence and obligation are given to the National Assembly by the Constitution. The Constitution also makes it clear that municipalities can be established only on the basis of law and not on the basis of a decision. So it is the law that lays the foundation for the establishment of congregations. The law can only be adopted by the National Assembly and no other body or institution in our country. The adoption of any law is both a professional and a political act, and the establishment of the congregation is also an expert and political decision that can only be taken by the National Assembly. By a decision of the Constitutional Court, for which Members and Members have tabled a declaration, constitutional principles have been violated. The principle of power-sharing, the principle of the rule of law, the principle of democratic human power, which Members and Members must not allow. For all the reasons set out in the declaration, the National Assembly cannot, in any respect, implement the Constitutional Court decision establishing the municipality of Ankara, neither by legislative nor by other measures. This means that it insists on the valid legal designation of the territory of the Republic of Slovenia, so that the elections can be declared only in the municipalities established by the Law establishing the municipalities and establishing their areas, which was first published in the Official Journal of the Republic of Slovenia. 108/06-ZUDNO-UPB1 – I say this because of the magnetogram – and later in the Official Journal of the Republic of Slovenia, No. 9/2011-ZUDNO-G. Your Honor! I expect that we will support the declaration and thus allow the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia to continue to carry out the sovereign, independent and responsible tasks entrusted to it by the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u164	The Committee on Home Affairs, Public Administration and Local Self-Government considered, at the first meeting below, the draft declaration on constitutional aspects of the implementation of the Constitutional Court decision as the parent working body. The Committee was informed of the proposal for a declaration published in the Rapporteur, the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service, the opinion of the Municipality of Koper, the opinion of the Coastal Self-Governmental Community of Italian Nationalities and the opinion of the Municipality of Ankara in the constitution. In the course of the course of business, the Parliamentary Group of Positive Slovenia tabled the text of the amendment declaration. In an additional explanation for the parts of the draft declaration, the representative of the proposaler, Jožef Kavtičnik, stressed that the proposed declaration advocated the defence of the Constitution and the integrity of the National Assembly, since the Constitutional Court, in the opinion of the applicants, had seriously infringed the legislative authority because in principle of the division of power, the rule of law and the principle of democracy. He pointed out that the inaction of the National Assembly in relation to that decision of the Constitutional Court would constitute the abandonment of a legislative function to the Constitutional Court. He also pointed out that the Constitutional Court, by its decision, thus not only circumvented the will of the National Assembly, but also the will of the State Council, and created a precedent in the establishment of municipalities. He also presented his opinion by constitutional lawyer Dr Ivan Kristan, who pointed out that the Constitutional Court’s decision, if applicable, would have negative consequences on Slovenia as a legal state, as it was his view that the Constitutional Court would decide against the Constitution and put itself on the Council of State, thereby jeopardising the principle of the division of power and the principle of the rule of law. He pointed out that the National Assembly had acted in accordance with the requirements of the Constitutional Court in the first decision but had decided negatively. The Constitutional Court then ruled against the legislator's will, violating Article 139 of the Constitution in his view. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service explained the opinion of the Service, in which he noted that the declaration was substantively similar to the motion for a resolution three years ago. It presented aspects of the legal nature of the declaration, the principles of the division of power and the powers of the President of the National Assembly. It pointed out that the declaration was not a legally binding act which did not regulate individual relations but had a general effect and that the adoption of the declaration would therefore call for non-compliance with the decisions of the Constitutional Court. The Constitutional Court considers that it is independent and independent and that its decisions are final, authoritative and compulsory, so the National Assembly must not interfere with the work and decision of the Constitutional Court. She also warned that the decision in the failure to sign the elections of the municipality of Ankara would be unconstitutional and illegal. The Legislative and Legal Service therefore notes that the proposal for a declaration is not substantively appropriate for adoption. The representative of the proposer reiterated that the municipality could only be established by law and pointed out that violations of the electoral right of residents in the Ankaran municipality would not occur in the constitution, as they could be carried out within the Municipality of Koper. In his speech, the representative of the Association of Municipal municipalities of Slovenia pointed out that the establishment of new municipalities in the absence of a second level of local self-government makes no sense. In his view, in the present case, we are faced with a lack of respect for the principle of the division of power. By the first decision in which the Constitution was established, the Constitutional Court was to destroy the role of the State Council, and by the second decision in which the Constitutional Court established a way to eliminate the anti-constitutionality, the role of the National Assembly. The Committee considered the proposal for a declaration on the basis of the reasonable application of the provisions of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly governing the second reading of the draft law, as provided for in Article 171 thereof.
