ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u172	Dear Madam Vice President, dear colleague, colleague! As we have already heard from the presentations made so far on the opinions of the Group members, this is an extremely complex issue. The Declaration on Constitutional Aspects of the Enforcement of the Decision of the Constitutional Court of the Municipality of Ankara of 9 June 2011 is a document which concerns not only the Municipality of Ankaran or the Municipality of Koper, but the whole of Slovenia and the gold in the opinion of Positive Slovenia each of us, Members and Members, as they speak of respect for the constitutional position and legislative functions of the National Assembly. It is a reaction to a decision taken three years ago by the Constitutional Court, to which the National Assembly has not yet formally reacted. In the Parliamentary Group of Positive Slovenia, which was a party that had not yet been to Parliament, we decided that the constitutional position of the National Assembly should be protected, with signatures supported by a number of Members and Members from some other parties. This declaration is important because it establishes a relationship between different authorities. As I said, this is a very difficult legal issue, on which the greatest experts in constitutional law – not all lawyers, but experts in constitutional law – are, with the exception of one, completely uniform. Type of constitutional lawyers, Prof. Dr. Janez Čebulj, Dr. Ivan Kristan, Prof. Dr. Franz Grad, Dr. Miro Cerar and Dr. Janez Šmidovnik, in their professional opinions, point out that, in the event of the establishment of the municipality of Ankaran, the Constitutional Court ruled for the first time in our history that the Constitutional Court went beyond the constitutional limits of the division of power. The Constitution provides that the Authority shall be exercised in accordance with the principle of the division of power over the legislative, executive and judicial authorities and that each constitutional authority shall act only within its own jurisdiction, with the Constitution of certain powers and shall not interfere with the powers of other authorities. Some constitutional lawyers are even sharper and say that the Constitutional Court has unlawfully adopted the role of legislator. The Court may, under Article 139 of the Constitution, be established only by law, and only the National Assembly and no one else may adopt laws under Articles 86 and 87 of the Constitution. Neither does the Constitutional Court. In our expert opinions, we also note the conclusion that not only the National Assembly, the Government, the President of the Republic and the Court are bound to respect the laws, but also the Constitutional Court, which is why the Constitution is not necessarily what the Constitutional Court says or decides, as stated by Dr. Miro Cerar, among other things. In Positive Slovenia, we have always respected the decisions of the Constitutional Court, but it is obvious this time that this is a mistake of the highest judicial authority and this has led us to a decision on this declaration. The Slovenian legal profession in the field of local self-government, Dr. Janez Šmidovnik, joins in the opinion of his colleagues and says that the Constitutional Court, by means of this decision, has seriously affected the geographical and economic integrity of the Municipality of Koper and, in particular, the port of Koper, which was divided into two almost equal halves between the municipality of Koper and Ankaran. "If such an arrangement were to be enforced," says Dr. Schmidtovnik, "the entire Slovenian economy would suffer as a result." In the PS Group, we do not want to define the content of this decision by means of a declaration, but we stress a very simple and understandable fact. If we want Members and Members to defend the legislative function and constitutional competence of the National Assembly, so that only he can take decisions on the establishment of municipalities, we will accept this declaration, otherwise we will knowingly leave the legislative function to the Constitutional Court and thereby give up our constitutional role and at the same time seriously intervene in the division of power in our country. The declaration that we are proposing will, of course, be broken.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u178	Madam Vice-President, colleagues and colleagues, thank you for your floor! In 2011, we adopted the Law on Social Entrepreneurship, prepared by the Members and filled the gap in the legislative framework of the operation and organisation of the third sector. The Social Entrepreneurship Act has laid the foundations for the development of social entrepreneurship, but for a greater role, for a greater number of social enterprises and consequently for an increase in the number of jobs in these companies, as well as an increase in the gross domestic product created from their activities, a supportive environment needs to be developed. In 2013, the Social Entrepreneurship Development Strategy 2013-2016 and the resulting Programme of Action for the implementation of the Strategy were launched. At the time of the debate on the adoption of the Social Entrepreneurship Act, we pointed out that a number of remote laws should also be amended. Thus, we have before us an example of the Law on Real Property of the State and Self-Governmental Local Communities, which sets out the treatment of movable and immovable property of the State and the self-governance of the local community and, of course, can have a significant impact on improving the working conditions of social enterprises. We have prepared a proposal for amendments to this law in autumn 2013, but we have, in agreement with the competent ministry, waited for their proposal for more comprehensive amendments to the Real Estate Act and the Local Self-Governmental Communities, which envisaged other solutions and placed in it articles allowing the free allocation of the premises of the State and the local community to social enterprises. Unfortunately, the Ministry was ahead of the early elections. That is why we have decided, even at the initiative of the local communities, that the content we originally wanted to include in this law, the amendment and the amendment of the law should be re- tabled by Members themselves in the reference to this Parliament. The basic objective of the proposed law is to allow free access to the immovable property of the State or the self-government local community to all entities with social enterprise status for a period not exceeding three years. After the expiry of this period, social enterprises should be given the opportunity to rent the same immovable property by entering into a direct contract. The law provides that a social undertaking must continue to operate in accordance with the principles of social entrepreneurship at the time of free use of the premises. In the event of a breach of this condition, the law provides for sanctions for the return of unjustifiable benefits in the amount of rent. Social enterprises in their operations pursue the objective of social benefit, fill gaps in the provision of social services and contribute to increasing employment opportunities for even more vulnerable categories of the population, which is particularly important for the achievement of the interests of local communities. We had to wait 30 days for the adoption of the law because of the business regulations. On the Committee, we have adopted amendments that have been corrected on the basis of the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service, where there have been mostly nomotechnical corrections, and we have also adopted some substantive supplements that now allow the law to be clearer, more operational and systemic, and to stress that I am particularly pleased that all the members on the Committee have been jointly supported by unanimous support. Colleagues and colleagues are again asking for support, as we are called upon to adopt the law primarily by local communities, who want the law to enter into force as soon as possible and to provide them with a legal basis for promoting social entrepreneurship in their environment, with the possibility of free allocation of premises in their possession, thereby creating new jobs and mitigating the social situation in their environment. Thank you very much.

Articles 29 and 30 of the Law on Real Estate of the State and Local Communities provide that real property may be leased on the basis of the direct contract method, provided that one of the following conditions is met. However, in the seventh indent, it provides that non-governmental organisations acting in the public interest may be leased in order to carry out an activity for which they are established or, hence, Article 30, so that immovable property which is temporarily not required by any user may be surrendered for free use by a direct contract to persons under public law for the performance of public functions or to non-governmental organisations acting in the public interest and others. In practice, the provisions have been unclear. By amending the law, we try to enable the rental contract to be concluded with the direct contract method when it comes to renting to social enterprises registered and operating in accordance with the law governing social entrepreneurship. We also want to allow the free use of state property or local self-governance, with the proposal also setting out in both cases the extent to which assets can be used under more favourable conditions. In both cases, it is the property necessary to carry out the activity for which a specific social undertaking is established. That the solution is the right one also proves the position of local communities. The Community of Slovene municipalities calls for the adoption of the law as soon as possible. The Community has been working in the area of the third sector for many years. The main aim is to increase the number of employees, to increase the integration of vulnerable groups into the labour market, and to expand quality services at local level. They therefore stress the need to provide the municipalities with individual tools to help new entrepreneurial ideas. The Association of Municipal municipalities of Slovenia also supports the bill as it follows the constitutional concept of local self-government and the principles of the European Charter on Local Self-Government. This provides for their appropriate decision-making freedom for local communities to adapt to the implementation of local circumstances. Therefore, colleagues, colleagues, I expect that today we will support the proposal of the law in its existing form and allow these entrepreneurial initiatives to have an additional potential for their development. In Positive Slovenia, we support the bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u190	Madam Vice-President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! First of all, I would like to point out that the bill before you today has been prepared in agreement with the unions, which we consider to be a particularly good result. As a result, the very content of the solutions is fully followed by both sides, both government and union. Why this marriage? The practice has shown that some issues relating to the return of overpayments of salaries, as regards inspection controls and the above limits on the amount of allowances, are not sufficiently regulated in the law, perhaps not regulated at all or are not regulated in an inadequate manner. Those shortcomings were also exposed by representative trade unions on the negotiating committee. We are pursuing three key objectives by means of this law. Firstly, establish a clear legal basis and procedure for the recovery of overpayments of salaries. Secondly, the abolition of the upper limit of the amount of allowances for less favourable working conditions and risks and of specific burdens which, under the current regime, must not exceed 20 % of the basic salary. Thirdly, the regulation of legal protection against records of inspections and the unequivocal determination of measures to be taken by public sector wage inspectors to remedy salary irregularities and to establish active legitimacy for objecting. As far as overpaid wages are concerned, the solutions are as follows. The general rules of civil law apply to the recovery of overpaid amounts of wages. Alternatively, in the event of an agreement, a public servant or an official shall pay only overpayments for a period of 10 months, the amount of reimbursement not exceeding twice the basic salary of a public official or full-time official for the month preceding the submission of the agreement. In the event of non-conclusion of the agreement, the employer shall require the refund of the full difference between the overpaid and the lawfully fixed remuneration before the competent court. For public servants who have in the month in which a proposal for a debt relief is made for a basic salary determined in accordance with Article 3, paragraph 3, of the law equal to or lower than the minimum wage, the debt shall be forgiven. The compensation liability of the person responsible for the exercise of the rights and duties of the employer shall be determined for the damage caused by the incorrect determination and payment of wages and the entities responsible for initiating the procedures for determining the compensation liability of that person. These are key points concerning this area. For the amount of additives as already mentioned, the overall upper limit of the amount of the allowances for less favourable working conditions and risks and special burdens has been lifted. However, the following solutions are crucial for inspection. First. There shall be a wide range of entities obliged by the inspector to send a record of the inspection carried out. Secondly, the amendment introduces an objection to the minutes as a form of legal protection against the minutes and an active legitimacy to lodge an objection. An objection to the minutes shall be decided by the Minister responsible for the system of salaries. The inspection procedures initiated prior to the entry into force of this Act shall be completed in accordance with the provisions of this amendment. A transitional period of two months has also been set since the entry into force of this amendment, in which the chairman of the budget user may lodge an objection to the minutes issued in the inspection procedures completed prior to the entry into force of this amendment to the Minister responsible for the system of wages. In short, honourable Members and Members so briefly on the solutions to this law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u200	Good evening, everyone in the hall! Let me go back a little bit to the past first. On 1 August 2008, a new salary system entered into force in the public sector, and employers also offered new employment contracts with revised wage levels. Most of the public servants of the annexes prepared by the employers did not even understand. They also did not understand the translation into the new pay system, with the employer in charge of translating and determining the legality and payment of salaries. The invalidity of contractual provisions relating to the determination of the salary of a public sector employee is thus caused by employers with insufficient due diligence in respect of compliance with mandatory standards which cannot be amended. Even though in the public sector the employer is solely in charge of ensuring the legality of the determination and payment of wages, the legislator introduced the concept of invalidity in the reverse direction, i.e. when it goes to a public official as a weaker party in this process to the obvious detriment. In doing so, the amount of indemnity laid down in the pay provision passed on to an employee who did not incur an invalidity and who could not affect the remuneration provision in any way. In doing so, the legislator has even established binding judicial protection, which means that the employer must recover from the public servant the amount overpaid on a judicial basis, if no method of return agreement is reached, under the threat of penalties, irrespective of the fact that the party has not infringed. We believe that when the government tries to resolve the situation in the manner set out in the proposal for this law, it recognises the error made by the implementation of the law on the public sector wage system, and we point out that the area governing civil servants' salaries needs to be re-examined and eliminated. In the Parliamentary Group of Positive Slovenia, we will support the bill mainly because it was prepared on the basis of a broad consensus with the public sector trade unions. At the time of the consideration of this law by the Committee on Home Affairs, Public Administration and Local Self-Government, we have already pointed out that this proposal unfortunately does not respect the constitutional principle of equality. The bill only regulates cases where the amounts of overpaid wages have not yet been repaid or have not been paid back in full. Since in individual cases the repayment of funds is already or is already completed, of course, the possibility is not excluded that the amount of funds recovered would exceed the amount that would have to be recovered on the basis of the agreement in accordance with Article 3a of the Law, and that is why some public servants could be in a worse position than those who have been delayed by the return of the money. In this way, we will today support the amendment that will allow these public servants to repay the overpaid money from the State. Thank you.

If there is no agreement, this case will be resolved before a court in accordance with civil law. ParlaMint-SI_2014-06-19-SDZ6-Izredna-70.ana.u208	Thank you again very much for your words, Madam Vice-President. Mr Secretary of State with a colleague, colleagues and colleagues! The reform of the pay system in the public sector in 2008, which simplified the way wages were determined across the public sector, was an admirable bargaining achievement at that time. The negotiations lasted for several years. In the public sector, wage chaos reigned until the single wage system was put in place. However, this important negotiating achievement has proved to be a large millstone in practice, which represents a huge additional burden on the state treasury since its entry into force. In addition to the fact that the implementation of the Single Wage System, which has greatly increased the budgetary expenditure for civil servants' salaries, coincided with the start of the financial and economic crisis that has affected Slovenia almost the most among EU countries, the implementation of the wage system has in practice caused a number of problems that we are still paying for. We are paying both taxpayers from the state budget – an example of this is the illegal suspension of payments of salary imbalances from 2010 – as well as public servants who have been paid too much wages for many years and are now obliged to pay them back. It's nothing to blame, but apparently a lot of debt. The proposed amendment to the Public Sector Wages Act is therefore the result of the long-term coordination of the Ministry of Public Administration with representative public sector trade unions, the result of which is a compromise solution concerning how to pay the pay gap in case of excessive or underpaid wages. The current regime, which provides for the recovery of overpaid salaries, makes it unclear that the obligations relating to recovery, return procedures and the obligation to file an action have been imposed, resulting in a variety of interpretations, unanimity of case law and consequently the different treatment of public servants. The proposed amendment to the law thus provides that, on the basis of an agreement between a public servant or official and an employer, a public servant or an official may pay only overpaid salaries for a period of ten months, without the amount of reimbursement exceeding twice the basic salary of a public official or a full-time official for a month prior to the submission of the agreement. However, if the agreement is not reached, the employer may request the refund of the full difference between the overpaid and the lawfully defined remuneration before the competent court. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian People's Party, we will not oppose the adoption of the proposed amendment to the Public Sector Wage System Act. However, we are asked why only a public servant and not an employer are responsible for the recovery of overpaid salaries. The latter, without the employee’s behaviour, led the payment procedures and paid the wages. Furthermore, in our view, the issue raised by the Legal and Legislative Service is also not resolved, namely the inequality in the situation of public servants who have already returned the difference in overpaid salaries or who are still returning this difference with public servants who will do so on the basis of the adoption of the proposed amendment to the law. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian People's Party, the proposed amendment to the Law on the System of Public Sector Wages will not be opposed. Thank you very much.
